# Proposed OEIS comments: A264360 to A267946

One section per OEIS entry: the date the result was obtained, the paper that proves it, what the entry still records as unsettled, and the text proposed for posting on that entry.

**Nothing here has been posted.** See [../SUBMITTING.md](../SUBMITTING.md).

---

## A264360

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A264360>  
Result obtained: **1 September 2026**  
Paper: [1480](../papers/01001-01500/01480-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 3*a(n-1) + 28*a(n-2) + 57*a(n-3) + 10*a(n-4) - 24*a(n-5) + 8*a(n-6).
> Empirical g.f.: 2*x*(9 + 33*x + 36*x^2 - 13*x^3 - 6*x^4 + 4*x^5) / (1 - 3*x - 28*x^2 - 57*x^3 - 10*x^4 + 24*x^5 - 8*x^6). - _Colin Barker_, Jan 07 2019

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 1 024 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 5. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

> Empirical for column k:
> Empirical for row n:

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrences for columns k = 1, 2, 3 and 4 are true. Each column counts arrays of a fixed width, and counting those a row at a time gives a transfer matrix, so every column satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most the number of states; checking the ones above is then a finite exact computation, and they hold. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A264509

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A264509>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [635](../papers/00501-01000/00635-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 69 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 256 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 69, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A264572

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A264572>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [677](../papers/00501-01000/00677-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 56 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 1024 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 56, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A265922

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A265922>  
Result obtained: **7 September 2026**  
Paper: [9483](../papers/09001-09500/09483-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 5*a(n-1) +110*a(n-2) +186*a(n-3) -1189*a(n-4) -2127*a(n-5) +4920*a(n-6) +5776*a(n-7) -9216*a(n-8)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. The condition is carried by a bounded amount of state down the array -- one bit per column where it asks for a parity reaching back to the top, and otherwise a window of three consecutive lines, since every cell it names lies one step away -- so counting the arrays a line at a time gives a transfer matrix with 11 states, and a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 8. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A266063

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A266063>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [11478](../papers/11001-11500/11478-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 78 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 97 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 78, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A267638

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A267638>  
Result obtained: **3 September 2026**  
Paper: [3836](../papers/03501-04000/03836-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = a(n-1) +6*a(n-2) -6*a(n-3) -8*a(n-4) +8*a(n-5).
> Empirical: G.f.: -2*x*(1+3*x-x^2-2*x^3+2*x^4) / ( (x-1)*(2*x+1)*(2*x-1)*(2*x^2-1) ). - _R. J. Mathar_, Jan 26 2016

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 40 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 4. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

---

## A267641

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A267641>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [11338](../papers/11001-11500/11338-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 93 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 2619 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 93, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A267790

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A267790>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [11341](../papers/11001-11500/11341-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 93 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 589 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 93, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A267867

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A267867>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [4715](../papers/04501-05000/04715-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Jan 21 2016 and Apr 20 2019: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 4 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A267920>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [517](../papers/00501-01000/00517-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Jan 22 2016 and Apr 20 2019: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence follows from the generating function this entry already states as fact: a recurrence with constant coefficients is exactly a denominator of the generating function, so the two are the same statement, and it holds for n > 2. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A267928

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A267928>  
Result obtained: **3 September 2026**  
Paper: [3274](../papers/03001-03500/03274-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 12*a(n-1) - 48*a(n-2) + 44*a(n-3) + 150*a(n-4) - 252*a(n-5) - 216*a(n-6) + 324*a(n-7) + 243*a(n-8).
> Empirical g.f.: x*(4 - 32*x + 60*x^2 + 94*x^3 - 284*x^4 - 152*x^5 + 388*x^6 + 259*x^7) / ((1 + x)^3*(1 - 3*x)^5). - _Colin Barker_, Feb 25 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 21 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 8. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

---

## A267929

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A267929>  
Result obtained: **3 September 2026**  
Paper: [2405](../papers/02001-02500/02405-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 65*a(n-1) -1714*a(n-2) +21994*a(n-3) -104609*a(n-4) -629015*a(n-5) +9424776*a(n-6) -16529832*a(n-7) -255376098*a(n-8) +1037637378*a(n-9) +3611188980*a(n-10) -20939962212*a(n-11) -32646831786*a(n-12) +213582240666*a(n-13) +202683094344*a(n-14) -1065454174440*a(n-15) -388324470141*a(n-16) +2871948084957*a(n-17) -843801825042*a(n-18) -3200868080118*a(n-19) +3106724901291*a(n-20) -847288609443*a(n-21)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 401 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 21. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```
